,
43
]

\{\mu_{m_{1},a}^{Y_{1}}:(m_{1},a)\in[2^{nR_{1}}]\times\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon}^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}\}
as

\displaystyle\mu_{m_{1},a}^{Y_{1}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\left(\sum_{\widehat{m}_{1},\widehat{b}_{1},\widehat{a}}\gamma^{*}_{(v_{1}^{n},d_{1}^{n})(\widehat{b}_{1},\widehat{m}_{1}),(u^{n},d_{2}^{n})(\widehat{a})}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{b_{1}}\gamma^{*}_{(v_{1}^{n},d_{1}^{n})(b_{1},m_{1}),(u^{n},d_{2}^{n})(a)}\right)\left(\sum_{\widehat{m}_{1},\widehat{b}_{1},\widehat{a}}\gamma^{*}_{(v_{1}^{n},d_{1}^{n})(\widehat{b}_{1},\widehat{m}_{1}),(u^{n},d_{2}^{n})(\widehat{a})}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\mbox{and }\mu^{Y_{1}}_{-1}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}I^{Y_{1}}-\sum_{m_{1}}\sum_{a}\mu^{Y_{1}}_{m_{1},a},\mbox{ where }

\displaystyle\gamma^{*}_{(v_{1}^{n},d_{1}^{n})(b_{1},m_{1}),(u^{n},d_{2}^{n})(a)}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\left(I_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{H}_{Y_{1}}^{e}}}-\beta^{*}_{(v_{1}^{n},d_{1}^{n})(b_{1},m_{1}),(u^{n},d_{2}^{n})(a)}\right)\pi_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{H}_{Y_{1}}^{G}}}\left(I_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{H}_{Y_{1}}^{e}}}-\beta^{*}_{(v_{1}^{n},d_{1}^{n})(b_{1},m_{1}),(u^{n},d_{2}^{n})(a)}\right).

(11)

Decoding POVM for Rxs
2
and
3
:
Having described the decoding POVM for Rx
1
, we now turn to the decoding POVMs for Rx
2
and Rx
3
. Since their constructions are essentially identical, we present only the decoding POVM for Rx
2
.
Rx
2
aims to decode the message
m_{2}=(m_{21},m_{22})
corresponding to the codewords
u_{2}^{n}(a_{2}(\underline{m}),m_{21})
and
v_{2}^{n}(b_{2}(\underline{m}),m_{22})
. We adopt the joint decoding strategy proposed in
[
23
]
. To this end, we first define the associated classical-quantum state

\displaystyle\rho^{U_{2}V_{2}Y_{2}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\sum_{u_{2}}\sum_{v_{2}}p_{U_{2}V_{2}}(u_{2},v_{2})\outerproduct{u_2,v_2}{u_2,v_2}\otimes\rho^{Y_{2}}_{u_{2},v_{2}},\mbox{ where}

(12)

\displaystyle\rho^{Y_{2}}_{u_{2},v_{2}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\sum_{v_{1},u_{3},v_{3}}p_{V_{1}U_{3}V_{3}|U_{2}V_{2}}(v_{1},u_{3},v_{3}|u_{2},v_{2})\rho^{Y_{2}}_{f(u_{2},u_{3},\underline{v})}.

We define the marginal density operators as

\displaystyle\rho^{Y_{2}}_{u_{2}}=\sum_{v_{2}}p_{V_{2}|U_{2}}(v_{2}|u_{2})\rho^{Y_{2}}_{u_{2},v_{2}},\>\rho^{Y_{2}}_{v_{2}}=\sum_{u_{2}}p_{U_{2}|V_{2}}(u_{2}|v_{2})\rho^{Y_{2}}_{u_{2},v_{2}},\mbox{ and }\rho^{Y_{2}}=\sum_{u_{2}}\sum_{v_{2}}p_{U_{2}V_{2}}(u_{2},v_{2})\rho^{Y_{2}}_{u_{2},v_{2}}.

(13)

Let
\pi^{Y_{2}}_{u_{2}^{n},v_{2}^{n}}
,
\pi^{Y_{2}}_{u_{2}^{n}}
,
\pi^{Y_{2}}_{v_{2}^{n}}
and
\pi^{Y_{2}}
be the C-Typ-Proj
with respect to the states
\rho^{Y_{2}}_{u_{2}^{n},v_{2}^{n}}=\bigotimes_{t=1}^{n}\rho^{Y_{2}}_{u_{2_{t}},v_{2_{t}}}
,
\rho^{Y_{2}}_{u_{2}^{n}}=\bigotimes_{t=1}^{n}\rho^{Y_{2}}_{u_{2_{t}}}
,
\rho^{Y_{2}}_{v_{2}^{n}}=\bigotimes_{t=1}^{n}\rho^{Y_{2}}_{v_{2_{t}}}
, and
\bigotimes_{t=1}^{n}\rho^{Y_{2}}
respectively. Define the operator

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21}),v_{2}^{n}(b_{2},m_{22})}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\pi^{Y_{2}}\>\pi^{Y_{2}}_{u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21})}\>\pi^{Y_{2}}_{u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21}),v_{2}^{n}(b_{2},m_{22})}\>\pi^{Y_{2}}_{u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21})}\pi^{Y_{2}}.

(14)

Finally, we define the square-root measurement
[
25
,
43
]

\{\mu^{Y_{2}}_{m_{2}}:m_{2}\in[2^{nR_{2}}]\}
as

\displaystyle\hskip-18.06749pt\mu^{Y_{2}}_{m_{2}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\left(\sum_{\tilde{m}_{22},\tilde{b_{2}},\tilde{m}_{21},\tilde{a}_{2}}\Upsilon_{u_{2}^{n}(\tilde{a}_{2},\tilde{m}_{21}),v_{2}^{n}(\tilde{b_{2}},\tilde{m}_{22})}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{b_{2},a_{2}}\Upsilon_{u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21}),v_{2}^{n}(b_{2},m_{22})}\right)\left(\sum_{\tilde{m}_{22},\tilde{b_{2}},\tilde{m}_{21},\tilde{a}_{2}}\Upsilon_{u_{2}^{n}(\tilde{a}_{2},\tilde{m}_{21}),v_{2}^{n}(\tilde{b_{2}},\tilde{m}_{22})}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\hskip-18.06749pt\mbox{and }\mu_{-1}^{Y_{2}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}I^{Y_{2}}-\sum_{m_{2}}\mu_{m_{2}}^{Y_{2}}.

Error analysis:

As is standard, we derive an upper bound on the error probability of a good code by averaging the error probability over an ensemble of codes. We begin by specifying the codebook distribution, i.e, the random code distribution, with respect to which this averaging is performed. Towards that end, recall that
g_{j}=\left[\!\!\begin{array}[]{c}g_{I}^{j}\\
g_{O/I}^{j}\end{array}\!\!\right]
for
j=2,3
and these generator matrices satisfy
g_{\mathsf{b}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\left[\!\!\begin{array}[]{c}g_{\mathsf{s}}\\
g_{\Delta}\end{array}\!\!\right]
, where
\mathsf{b}
is defined as in (
6
). From the code structure, encoding and decoding it is evident that our coding strategy is completely specified via the following objects :
c_{1}=(v_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}):b_{1}\in[2^{nK_{1}}],m_{1}\in[2^{nR_{1}}]),c_{jj}=\{v_{j}^{n}(b_{j},m_{jj}):b_{j}\in[2^{nK_{j}}],m_{jj}\in[2^{nL_{j}}]\}
for
j\in\{2,3\}
,
g_{\mathsf{b}}
,
\mathtt{b}_{j}^{n}
for
j\in\{2,3\}
,
(d_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}):b_{1}\in[2^{nK_{1}}],m_{1}\in[2^{nR_{1}}])),(d_{2}^{n}(a):a\in\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon}^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}))
,
b_{j}:[2^{nR_{1}}]\times[2^{nR_{2}}]\times[2^{nR_{3}}]\rightarrow[2^{nK_{j}}]
, for
j\in[3]
and finally maps
a_{j}:[2^{nR_{1}}]\times[2^{nR_{2}}]\times[2^{nR_{3}}]\rightarrow[2^{nS_{j}}]
for
j\in\{2,3\}
. It suffices therefore to specify the joint distribution of these objects. For any choice of the arguments in their respective range spaces, let

\displaystyle\hskip-21.68121ptP\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\left(V_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1})=v_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}):b_{1}\in[2^{nK_{1}}],m_{1}\in[2^{nR_{1}}]\right),\left(\left(V_{j}^{n}(b_{j},m_{jj}):b_{j}\in[2^{nK_{j}}],m_{jj}\in[2^{nL_{j}}]\right):j=2,3\right),\\
G_{b}=g_{b},\left(\mathtt{B}_{j}^{n}=\mathtt{b}_{j}^{n}:j=2,3\right),\left(D_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1})=d_{1}^{n}:b_{1}\in[2^{nK_{1}}],m_{1}\in[2^{nR_{1}}]\right),\left(D_{2}^{n}(a)=d_{2}^{n}:a\in\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon}^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}\right),\\
\left(\left(B_{j}(\underline{m})=b_{j}(\underline{m}):\underline{m}\in[2^{nR_{1}}]\times[2^{nR_{2}}]\times[2^{nR_{3}}]\right):j\in[3]\right),\\
\left(\left(A_{j}(\underline{m})=a_{j}(\underline{m}):\underline{m}\in[2^{nR_{1}}]\times[2^{nR_{2}}]\times[2^{nR_{3}}]\right):j=2,3\right)\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-21.68121pt=\left[\prod_{m_{1}=1}^{2^{nR_{1}}}\prod_{b_{1}=1}^{2^{nK_{1}}}p_{V_{1}}^{n}(v_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}))\right]\left[\prod_{j=2}^{3}\prod_{m_{jj}=1}^{2^{nL_{j}}}\prod_{b_{j}=1}^{2^{nK_{j}}}p_{V_{j}}^{n}(v_{j}^{n}(b_{j},m_{jj}))\right]\frac{1}{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{2n}}\left[\prod_{m_{1}=1}^{2^{nR_{1}}}\prod_{b_{1}=1}^{2^{nK_{1}}}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}_{1}|^{n}}\right]\left[\prod_{a=1}^{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}_{2}|^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip-21.68121pt\left[\prod_{m_{1}=1}^{2^{nR_{1}}}\prod_{j=2}^{3}\prod_{m_{j1}=1}^{2^{nT_{j}}}\prod_{m_{jj}=1}^{2^{nL_{j}}}\frac{r_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n}(a_{2}(\underline{m}),m_{21}),u_{3}^{n}(a_{3}(\underline{m}),m_{31}),v_{1}^{n}(b_{1}(\underline{m}),m_{1}),v_{2}^{n}(b_{2}(\underline{m}),m_{22}),v_{3}^{n}(b_{3}(\underline{m}),m_{33})\right)}{\sum_{\tilde{a}_{2},\tilde{a}_{3}}\sum_{\tilde{b}_{1},\tilde{b}_{2},\tilde{b}_{3}}r_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n}(\tilde{a}_{2},m_{21}),u_{3}^{n}(\tilde{a}_{3},m_{31}),v_{1}^{n}(\tilde{b}_{1},m_{1}),v_{2}^{n}(\tilde{b}_{2},m_{22}),v_{3}^{n}(\tilde{b}_{3},m_{33})\right)}\right]

(20)

specify our joint codebook distribution, where

\displaystyle r_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21}),u_{3}^{n}(a_{3},m_{31}),v_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),v_{2}^{n}(b_{2},m_{22}),v_{3}^{n}(b_{3},m_{33})\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\frac{p_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21}),u_{3}^{n}(a_{3},m_{31}),v_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),v_{2}^{n}(b_{2},m_{22}),v_{3}^{n}(b_{3},m_{33})\right)}{q^{n}_{U_{2}}(u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21}))q^{n}_{U_{3}}(u_{3}^{n}(a_{3},m_{31}))p_{V_{1}}^{n}(v_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}))p_{V_{2}}^{n}(v_{2}^{n}(b_{2},m_{22}))p_{V_{3}}^{n}(v_{3}^{n}(b_{3},m_{33}))}

and for
j=2,3
,
q_{U_{j}}^{n}(u_{j}^{n})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{n}}
is the uniform distribution.
We now derive an upper bound on the average error probability of a good code, by averaging the error probability of every code with respect to the above specified codebook distribution. Note that, upon receiving the quantum state
\rho^{Y_{1}}_{x^{n}(\underline{m})},

Rx 1 prepares the auxiliary state
\outerproduct{0}{0}
, concatenates the same with the received state and measures this concatenated state via the square root measurement
[
25
,
43
]

\{\mu^{Y_{1}}_{m_{1},a}:(m_{1},a)\in[2^{nR_{1}}]\times\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon}^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}\}
on the combined state
\left(\rho^{Y_{1}}_{x^{n}(\underline{m})}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}\right)
.
Hence, the average error probability of the code is given by

\displaystyle\hskip-21.68121pt\mathbf{P}(e,\uline{\mu})=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\sum_{\underline{m}}\sum_{a}\tr\left\{\left(I^{Y_{1}Y_{2}Y_{3}}-\mu^{Y_{1}}_{m_{1},a}\otimes\mu_{m_{2}}^{Y_{2}}\otimes\mu_{m_{3}}^{Y_{3}}\right)\left(\rho_{x^{n}(\underline{m})}^{Y_{1}Y_{2}Y_{3}}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}\right)\right\}.

Let
E
and
F
be operators on the quantum systems
A
and
B
, respectively, satisfying
0\leq E\leq I^{A}
and
0\leq F\leq I^{B}
. Then, observe that

\displaystyle I^{AB}-E\otimes F

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(I^{A}-E)\otimes F+I^{A}\otimes(I^{B}-F)

(21)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(I^{A}-E)\otimes I^{B}+I^{AB}-(I^{A}\otimes F).

From (
21
), we have

\displaystyle I^{Y_{1}Y_{2}Y_{3}}-\mu^{Y_{1}}_{m_{1},a}\otimes\mu_{m_{2}}^{Y_{2}}\otimes\mu_{m_{3}}^{Y_{3}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(I^{Y_{1}Y_{2}Y_{3}}-\mu^{Y_{1}}_{m_{1},a}\otimes I^{Y_{2}}\otimes I^{Y_{3}}\right)+\left(I^{Y_{1}Y_{2}Y_{3}}-I^{Y_{1}}\otimes\mu_{m_{2}}^{Y_{2}}\otimes I^{Y_{3}}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(I^{Y_{1}Y_{2}Y_{3}}-I^{Y_{1}}\otimes I^{Y_{2}}\otimes\mu_{m_{3}}^{Y_{3}}\right).

Using the above inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(e,\uline{\mu})\leq T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3},\mbox{ where }

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\sum_{\underline{m}}\sum_{a}\tr\left\{\left(I-\mu^{Y_{1}}_{m_{1},a}\right)\left(\rho_{x^{n}(\underline{m})}^{Y_{1}}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}\right)\right\},

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\sum_{\underline{m}}\tr\left\{\left(I-\mu_{m_{2}}^{Y_{2}}\right)\rho_{x^{n}(\underline{m})}^{Y_{2}}\right\},

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\sum_{\underline{m}}\tr\left\{\left(I-\mu_{m_{3}}^{Y_{3}}\right)\rho_{x^{n}(\underline{m})}^{Y_{3}}\right\}.

Rx
1
’s Error Analysis
: We begin by analyzing the first term
T_{1}
, which corresponds to Rx
1
’s error analysis. Observe that

\displaystyle\hskip 166.2212pt\mu_{m_{1},a}^{Y_{1}}\geq\Gamma^{Y_{1}}_{m_{1},a},\mbox{ where}

\displaystyle\Gamma^{Y_{1}}_{m_{1},a}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\left(\sum_{\widehat{m}_{1},\widehat{b}_{1},\widehat{a}}\gamma^{*}_{(v_{1}^{n},d_{1}^{n})(\widehat{b}_{1},\widehat{m}_{1}),(u^{n},d_{2}^{n})(\widehat{a})}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\gamma^{*}_{(v_{1}^{n},d_{1}^{n})(b_{1}(\underline{m}),m_{1}),(u^{n},d_{2}^{n})(a)}\left(\sum_{\widehat{m}_{1},\widehat{b}_{1},\widehat{a}}\gamma^{*}_{(v_{1}^{n},d_{1}^{n})(\widehat{b}_{1},\widehat{m}_{1}),(u^{n},d_{2}^{n})(\widehat{a})}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}},

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\sum_{\underline{m}}\sum_{a}\tr\left\{\left(I-\Gamma^{Y_{1}}_{m_{1},a}\right)\left(\rho_{x^{n}(\underline{m})}^{Y_{1}}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\sum_{\underline{m}}\sum_{a}\sum_{a_{2},a_{3}}\sum_{b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}}\mathds{1}\left\{{a_{i}(\underline{m})=a_{i}:i=2,3,b_{j}(\underline{m})=b_{j}:j\in[3]}\right\}\tr\left\{\left(I-\Gamma^{Y_{1}}_{m_{1},a}\right)\left(\rho_{x^{n}(\underline{m})}^{Y_{1}}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}\right)\!\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{=}

\displaystyle T_{1.1}+T_{1.2},\mbox{ where}

\displaystyle T_{1.1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\sum_{\underline{m}}\sum_{a}\sum_{a_{2},a_{3}}\sum_{b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}}\tr\left\{\left(I-\Gamma^{Y_{1}}_{m_{1},a}\right)\left(\rho_{x^{n}(\underline{m})}^{Y_{1}}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip-46.97505pt\left[\begin{aligned} \mathds{1}\left\{a_{i}(\underline{m})=a_{i}:i=2,3,b_{j}(\underline{m})=b_{j}:j\in[3]\right\}-\frac{r_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21}),u_{3}^{n}(a_{3},m_{31}),v_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),v_{2}^{n}(b_{2},m_{22}),v_{3}^{n}(b_{3},m_{33})\right)}{2^{n(S_{2}+S_{3}+K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3})}}\end{aligned}\right],

\displaystyle T_{1.2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\frac{1}{2^{n(S_{2}+S_{3}+K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3})}}\sum_{\underline{m}}\sum_{a}\sum_{a_{2},a_{3}}\sum_{b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}}\tr\left\{\left(I-\Gamma^{Y_{1}}_{m_{1},a}\right)\left(\rho_{x^{n}(\underline{m})}^{Y_{1}}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle r_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21}),u_{3}^{n}(a_{3},m_{31}),v_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),v_{2}^{n}(b_{2},m_{22}),v_{3}^{n}(b_{3},m_{33})\right).

The equality
(*)
follows by adding and subtracting

\displaystyle\frac{r_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21}),u_{3}^{n}(a_{3},m_{31}),v_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),v_{2}^{n}(b_{2},m_{22}),v_{3}^{n}(b_{3},m_{33})\right)}{2^{n(S_{2}+S_{3}+K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3})}},

thereby decomposing the expression into two terms
T_{1.1}
and
T_{1.2}
described above.
The term
T_{1.1}
is simply bounded by ‘cloud mixing’
[
33
]
. We provide the following proposition.

Proposition 4
.

For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, and for all
\delta
sufficiently small and
n
sufficiently large, we have
\mathbb{E}[T_{1.1}]\leq\epsilon
if

\displaystyle S_{\mathcal{A}}+K_{\mathcal{B}}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle|\mathcal{A}|\log\left(\upsilon\right)+\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}H(V_{\beta})-H(U_{\mathcal{A}},V_{\mathcal{B}}),

\displaystyle\max\{S_{2}+T_{2},S_{3}+T_{3}\}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\log(\Prime)-\min_{\theta\in\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon}\setminus\{0\}}H(U_{2}\oplus\theta U_{3}),\mbox{ and }

\displaystyle K_{\mathcal{B}}+\max\{S_{2}+T_{2},S_{3}+T_{3}\}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\log(\Prime)+\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}H(V_{\beta})-\min_{\theta\in\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon}\setminus\{0\}}H(U_{2}\oplus\theta U_{3},V_{\mathcal{B}}),

where
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\{2,3\}
,
\mathcal{B}\subseteq\{1,2,3\}
,
S_{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}S_{\alpha}
,
U_{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(U_{\alpha}:\alpha\in\mathcal{A})
,
K_{\mathcal{B}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}K_{\beta}
and
V_{\mathcal{B}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(V_{\beta}:\beta\in\mathcal{B})
.

Proof.

See Appendix
B
for a proof.
∎

Next, to bound the term
T_{1.2}
, we first employ an alternate ‘proxy’ state. Specifically, we substitute the original received state (
\rho_{v_{1}^{n},u^{n}}^{Y_{1}}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}
) by a specific ‘tilted state’. The effect of this substitution on the error probability can be suppressed by ensuring that the tilted state is close to the original received state in the
\mathbb{L}_{1}-
norm . Towards identifying this tilted state, we define a new titling map

\mathcal{T}_{d_{1}^{n},d_{2}^{n},\eta}:\boldsymbol{\mathcal{H}_{Y_{1}}^{G}}\rightarrow\boldsymbol{\mathcal{H}_{Y_{1}}^{e}}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{d_{1}^{n},d_{2}^{n},\eta}(\ket{h})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+2\eta^{2}}}\left(\ket{h}+\eta\ket{h}\otimes\ket{d_{1}^{n}}+\eta\ket{h}\otimes\ket{d_{2}^{n}}\right),

to tilt the state in all directions. As discussed earlier, this tilting map is chosen carefully so as to ensure that the tilted state remains close to the original state in
\mathbb{L}_{1}
norm. Proposition
5
establishes this thereby guaranteeing that the two states induce approximately the same measurement outcome statistics. Let

\displaystyle\theta_{v_{1}^{n},d_{1}^{n},u^{n},d_{2}^{n}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathcal{T}_{d_{1}^{n},d_{2}^{n},\eta}\left(\rho^{Y_{1}}_{v_{1}^{n},u^{n}}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}\right),

(22)

be the tilted state along all directions, where
\mathcal{T}_{d_{1}^{n},d_{2}^{n},\eta}
acts on each pure state in the mixture individually.

Proposition 5
.

For n sufficiently large, we have

\displaystyle\norm{\theta_{v_1^n,d_1^n,u^n,d_2^n} - \left( \rho_{v_1^n,u^n}^{Y_1} \otimes\ketbra{0}\right) }_{1}\leq 4\eta.

Proof.

The proof is provided in Appendix
C
.
∎

Now, we evaluate the expectation with respect to the random choice of the codebook. We obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[T_{1.2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\frac{1}{2^{n(S_{2}+S_{3}+K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3})}}\sum_{\underline{m}}\sum_{a}\sum_{a_{2},a_{3}}\sum_{b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}}\sum_{u_{2}^{n}}\sum_{u_{3}^{n}}\sum_{\underline{v}^{n}}\sum_{u^{n}}q_{U_{2}}^{n}(u_{2}^{n})q_{U_{3}}^{n}(u_{3}^{n})p_{V_{1}}^{n}(v_{1}^{n})p_{V_{1}}^{n}(v_{2}^{n})p_{V_{3}}^{n}(v_{3}^{n})

(23)

\displaystyle r^{n}_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}(u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n},\underline{v}^{n})\mathds{1}\{u^{n}=u_{2}^{n}\oplus u_{3}^{n}\}\tr\left\{\left(I-\Gamma^{Y_{1}}_{m_{1},a}\right)\left(\rho_{f^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n},\underline{v}^{n}\right)}^{Y_{1}}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\frac{1}{2^{n(S_{2}+S_{3}+K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3})}}\sum_{\underline{m}}\sum_{a}\sum_{a_{2},a_{3}}\sum_{b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}}\sum_{u_{2}^{n}}\sum_{u_{3}^{n}}\sum_{\underline{v}^{n}}\sum_{u^{n}}p_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}(u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n},\underline{v}^{n})\mathds{1}\{u^{n}=u_{2}^{n}\oplus u_{3}^{n}\}

\displaystyle\tr\left\{\left(I-\Gamma^{Y_{1}}_{m_{1},a}\right)\left(\rho_{f^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n},\underline{v}^{n}\right)}^{Y_{1}}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\frac{1}{2^{n(S_{2}+S_{3}+K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3})}}\sum_{\underline{m}}\sum_{a}\sum_{a_{2},a_{3}}\sum_{b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}}\sum_{u_{2}^{n}}\sum_{u_{3}^{n}}\sum_{\underline{v}^{n}}\sum_{u^{n}}p_{U_{2},U_{3}\underline{V}U}^{n}(u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n},\underline{v}^{n},u^{n})

\displaystyle\tr\left\{\left(I-\Gamma^{Y_{1}}_{m_{1},a}\right)\left(\rho_{f^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n},\underline{v}^{n}\right)}^{Y_{1}}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{1}|}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\frac{1}{2^{nK_{1}}}\sum_{m_{1}}\sum_{a}\sum_{b_{1}}\sum_{v_{1}^{n}}\sum_{u^{n}}p_{V_{1}U}^{n}(v_{1}^{n},u^{n})\tr\left\{\left(I-\Gamma^{Y_{1}}_{m_{1},a}\right)\left(\rho_{v_{1}^{n},u^{n}}^{Y_{1}}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{1}|}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\frac{1}{2^{nK_{1}}}\sum_{m_{1}}\sum_{a}\sum_{b_{1}}\sum_{v_{1}^{n}}\sum_{u^{n}}p_{V_{1}U}^{n}(v_{1}^{n},u^{n})\norm{ \theta_{v_1^n,D_1^n(b_1,m_1),u^n,D_2^n(a)} - \left( \rho_{v_1^n,u^n}^{Y_1} \otimes\ketbra{0}\right)}_{1}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{1}|}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\frac{1}{2^{nK_{1}}}\sum_{m_{1}}\sum_{a}\sum_{b_{1}}\sum_{v_{1}^{n}}\sum_{u^{n}}p_{V_{1}U}^{n}(v_{1}^{n},u^{n})\tr\left\{\left(I-\Gamma^{Y_{1}}_{m_{1},a}\right)\theta_{v_{1}^{n},D_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),u^{n},D_{2}^{n}(a)}\right\}

where (a) follows from
q_{U_{2}}^{n}(u_{2}^{n})q_{U_{3}}^{n}(u_{3}^{n})p_{V_{1}}^{n}(v_{1}^{n})p_{V_{1}}^{n}(v_{2}^{n})p_{V_{3}}^{n}(v_{3}^{n})r^{n}_{U_{2}^{n}U_{3}^{n}\underline{V}^{n}}(u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n},\underline{v}^{n})=p_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}(u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n},\underline{v}^{n})
, (b) follows from
p^{n}_{U_{2},U_{3}\underline{V}U}(u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n},\underline{v}^{n},u^{n})=p^{n}_{U_{2},U_{3}\underline{V}}(u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n},\underline{v}^{n})\mathds{1}\{u^{n}=u_{2}^{n}\oplus u_{3}^{n}\}
, (c) follows by using the definition
\rho_{v_{1}^{n},u^{n}}^{Y_{1}}=\sum_{u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n}}\sum_{v_{2}^{n},v_{3}^{n}}p^{n}_{U_{2}U_{3}V_{2}V_{3}|V_{1}U}(u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n},v_{2}^{n},v_{3}^{n}|v_{1}^{n},u^{n})\rho^{Y_{1}}_{f^{n}(u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n},\underline{v}^{n})}
, and (d) follows from the trace inequality
\tr(\Delta\rho)\leq\tr(\Delta\sigma)+\frac{1}{2}\norm{\rho- \sigma}_{1}
, where
0\leq\Delta,\rho,\sigma\leq I
.

By evaluating the expectation over
(D_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),D_{2}^{n}(a))
and using Proposition
5
, we bound the first term in (
23
) by
4\eta
. Next, to bound the second term in (
23
), we apply Hayashi-Nagaoka inequality
[
34
]
. We obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[T_{1.2}]\leq 4\eta+2\>T_{1.2.1}+4\>\left(\sum_{i=2}^{6}T_{1.2.i}\right),\mbox{ where}

\displaystyle T_{1.2.1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{1}|}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\frac{1}{2^{nK_{1}}}\sum_{m_{1}}\sum_{a}\sum_{b_{1}}\sum_{v_{1}^{n}}\sum_{u^{n}}p_{V_{1}U}^{n}(v_{1}^{n},u^{n})\tr\left\{\left(I-\gamma^{*}_{v_{1}^{n},D_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),u^{n},D_{2}^{n}(a)}\right)\theta_{v_{1}^{n},D_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),u^{n},D_{2}^{n}(a)}\right\},

\displaystyle T_{1.2.2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{1}|}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\frac{1}{2^{nK_{1}}}\sum_{m_{1}}\sum_{a}\sum_{b_{1}}\sum_{\tilde{m}_{1}\neq m_{1}}\sum_{\tilde{b}_{1}}\sum_{v_{1}^{n}}\sum_{u^{n}}p_{V_{1}U}^{n}(v_{1}^{n},u^{n})

\displaystyle\tr\left\{\gamma^{*}_{(V_{1}^{n},D_{1}^{n})(\tilde{b}_{1},\tilde{m}_{1}),u^{n},D_{2}^{n}(a)}\>\theta_{v_{1}^{n},D_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),u^{n},D_{2}^{n}(a)}\right\}\!\!,

\displaystyle T_{1.2.3}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{1}|}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\frac{1}{2^{nK_{1}}}\sum_{m_{1}}\sum_{a}\sum_{b_{1}}\sum_{\tilde{a}\neq a}\sum_{v_{1}^{n}}\sum_{u^{n}}p_{V_{1}U}^{n}(v_{1}^{n},u^{n})\tr\left\{\gamma^{*}_{v_{1}^{n},D_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),(U^{n},D_{2}^{n})(\tilde{a})}\>\theta_{v_{1}^{n},D_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),u^{n},D_{2}^{n}(a)}\right\},

\displaystyle T_{1.2.4}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{1}|}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\frac{1}{2^{nK_{1}}}\sum_{m_{1}}\sum_{a}\sum_{b_{1}}\sum_{\tilde{m}_{1}\neq m_{1}}\sum_{\tilde{b}_{1}}\sum_{\tilde{a}\neq a}\sum_{v_{1}^{n}}\sum_{u^{n}}p_{V_{1}U}^{n}(v_{1}^{n},u^{n})

\displaystyle\tr\left\{\gamma^{*}_{(V_{1}^{n},D_{1}^{n})(\tilde{b}_{1},\tilde{m}_{1}),(U^{n},D_{2}^{n})(\tilde{a})}\>\theta_{v_{1}^{n},D_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),u^{n},D_{2}^{n}(a)}\right\},

\displaystyle T_{1.2.5}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{1}|}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\frac{1}{2^{nK_{1}}}\sum_{m_{1}}\sum_{a}\sum_{b_{1}}\sum_{\tilde{b}_{1}\neq b_{1}}\sum_{v_{1}^{n}}\sum_{u^{n}}p_{V_{1}U}^{n}(v_{1}^{n},u^{n})\tr\left\{\gamma^{*}_{(V_{1}^{n},D_{1}^{n})(\tilde{b}_{1},m_{1}),u^{n},D_{2}^{n}(a)}\>\theta_{v_{1}^{n},D_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),u^{n},D_{2}^{n}(a)}\right\},

\displaystyle T_{1.2.6}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{1}|}\frac{1}{\upsilon^{s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}}}\frac{1}{2^{nK_{1}}}\sum_{m_{1}}\sum_{a}\sum_{b_{1}}\sum_{\tilde{b}_{1}\neq b_{1}}\sum_{\tilde{a}\neq a}\sum_{v_{1}^{n}}\sum_{u^{n}}p_{V_{1}U}^{n}(v_{1}^{n},u^{n})

\displaystyle\tr\left\{\gamma^{*}_{(V_{1}^{n},D_{1}^{n})(\tilde{b}_{1},m_{1}),(U^{n},D_{2}^{n})(\tilde{a})}\>\theta_{v_{1}^{n},D_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),u^{n},D_{2}^{n}(a)}\right\}.

In the following propositions, we provide the rate constraints required to bound these error terms.

Proposition 6
.

For all
\epsilon>0
, and
\delta,\eta>0
sufficiently small and
n
sufficiently large, we have
\mathbb{E}[T_{1.2.1}]\leq\epsilon
.

Proof.

The proof is provided in Appendix
D
.
∎

Proposition 7
.

For any
\epsilon>0
, and for all
\delta,\eta>0
sufficiently small and
n
sufficiently large, we have
\sum_{i=2}^{6}\mathbb{E}[T_{1.2.i}]\leq\epsilon
if the following inequalities hold.

\displaystyle R_{1}+K_{1}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle H(V_{1})-H(V_{1}|Y_{1},U_{2}\oplus U_{3}),

\displaystyle\max\{S_{2}+T_{2},S_{3}+T_{3}\}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\log(\Prime)-H(U_{2}\oplus U_{3}|Y_{1},V_{1}),\mbox{ and}

\displaystyle R_{1}+K_{1}+\max\{S_{2}+T_{2},S_{3}+T_{3}\}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\log(\Prime)+H(V_{1})-H(V_{1},U_{2}\oplus U_{3}|Y_{1}),

where all the mutual information quantities are computed with respect to the state
\rho^{V_{1}U_{2}\oplus U_{3}Y_{1}}
defined in (
7
).

Proof.

The proof is provided in Appendix
E
.
∎

This completes analysis of error at Rx
1
. We now proceed to analyze
T_{2}
and
T_{3}
.

Rx
2
and
3
’s Error Analysis
: Since Rx
2
and
3
’s decoding POVMs and error probability terms are identical, we provide the analysis for Rx
2
. Consider the second term
T_{2}
, which corresponds to Rx
2
’s error analysis. Observe that

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\sum_{\underline{m}}\tr\left\{\left(I-\mu_{m_{2}}^{Y_{2}}\right)\rho_{x^{n}(\underline{m})}^{Y_{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\sum_{\underline{m}}\tr\left\{\left(I-\Gamma_{m_{2}}^{Y_{2}}\right)\rho_{x^{n}(\underline{m})}^{Y_{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\sum_{\underline{m}}\sum_{a_{2},a_{3}}\sum_{b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}}\mathds{1}\{a_{2}(\underline{m})=a_{2},a_{3}(\underline{m})=a_{3},b_{1}(\underline{m})=b_{1},b_{2}(\underline{m})=b_{2},b_{3}(\underline{m})=b_{3}\}\tr\left\{\left(I-\Gamma_{m_{2}}^{Y_{2}}\right)\rho_{x^{n}(\underline{m})}^{Y_{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{2.1}+T_{2.2},\mbox{ where}

\displaystyle T_{2.1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\sum_{\underline{m}}\sum_{a_{2},a_{3}}\sum_{b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}}\tr\left\{\left(I-\Gamma^{Y_{2}}_{m_{2}}\right)\rho_{x^{n}(\underline{m})}^{Y_{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip-43.36243pt\left[\begin{aligned} \mathds{1}\left\{a_{i}(\underline{m})=a_{i}:i=2,3,b_{j}(\underline{m})=b_{j}:j\in[3]\right\}-\frac{r_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21}),u_{3}^{n}(a_{3},m_{31}),v_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),v^{n}_{2}(b_{2},m_{22}),v^{n}_{3}(b_{3},m_{33})\right)}{2^{n(S_{2}+S_{3}+K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3})}}\end{aligned}\right]

\displaystyle T_{2.2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\frac{1}{2^{n(S_{2}+S_{3}+K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3})}}\sum_{\underline{m}}\sum_{a_{2},a_{3}}\sum_{b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}}\tr\left\{\left(I-\Gamma^{Y_{2}}_{m_{2}}\right)\rho_{x^{n}(\underline{m})}^{Y_{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle r_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21}),u_{3}^{n}(a_{3},m_{31}),v_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),v^{n}_{2}(b_{2},m_{22}),v^{n}_{3}(b_{3},m_{33})\right).

The first inequality follows from
\mu_{m_{2}}^{Y_{2}}\geq\Gamma_{m_{2}}^{Y_{2}}
, where

\displaystyle\Gamma_{m_{2}}^{Y_{2}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\left(\sum_{\tilde{m}_{21},\tilde{a}_{2},\tilde{m_{22}},\tilde{b}_{2}}\Upsilon_{u_{2}^{n}(\tilde{a}_{2},\tilde{m}_{21}),v_{2}^{n}(\tilde{b}_{2},\tilde{m}_{22})}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Upsilon_{u_{2}^{n}(a_{2}(\underline{m}),m_{21}),v_{2}^{n}(b_{2}(\underline{m}),m_{22})}\left(\sum_{\tilde{m}_{21},\tilde{a}_{2},\tilde{m_{22}},\tilde{b}_{2}}\Upsilon_{u_{2}^{n}(\tilde{a}_{2},\tilde{m}_{21}),v_{2}^{n}(\tilde{b}_{2},\tilde{m}_{22})}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

The last equality follows by adding and subtracting

\displaystyle\frac{r_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21}),u_{3}^{n}(a_{3},m_{31}),v_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),v^{n}_{2}(b_{2},m_{22}),v^{n}_{3}(b_{3},m_{33})\right)}{2^{n(S_{2}+S_{3}+K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3})}},

thereby decomposing the expression into two terms. The term
T_{2.1}
follows the same analysis as
T_{1.1}
using a cloud mixing argument. We state the following proposition.

Proposition 8
.

For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, and for all
\delta
sufficiently small and
n
sufficiently large, we have
\mathbb{E}[T_{2.1}]\leq\epsilon
if

\displaystyle S_{\mathcal{A}}+K_{\mathcal{B}}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle|\mathcal{A}|\log\left(\upsilon\right)+\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}H(V_{\beta})-H(U_{\mathcal{A}},V_{\mathcal{B}}),

\displaystyle\max\{S_{2}+T_{2},S_{3}+T_{3}\}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\log(\Prime)-\min_{\theta\in\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon}\setminus\{0\}}H(U_{2}\oplus\theta U_{3}),\mbox{ and}

\displaystyle K_{\mathcal{B}}+\max\{S_{2}+T_{2},S_{3}+T_{3}\}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\log(\Prime)+\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}H(V_{\beta})-\min_{\theta\in\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon}\setminus\{0\}}H(U_{2}\oplus\theta U_{3},V_{\mathcal{B}}),

where
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\{2,3\}
,
\mathcal{B}\subseteq\{1,2,3\}
,
S_{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}S_{\alpha}
,
U_{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(U_{\alpha}:\alpha\in\mathcal{A})
,
K_{\mathcal{B}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}K_{\beta}
and
V_{\mathcal{B}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(V_{\beta}:\beta\in\mathcal{B})
.

Proof.

The proof follows the same arguments as in Appendix
B
.
∎

We now evaluate the expectation of
T_{2.2}
with respect to the random codebook. We obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[T_{2.2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\frac{1}{2^{n(S_{2}+S_{3}+K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3})}}\sum_{\underline{m}}\sum_{a_{2},a_{3}}\sum_{b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}}\sum_{u_{2}^{n}}\sum_{u_{3}^{n}}\sum_{\underline{v}^{n}}q_{U_{2}}^{n}(u_{2}^{n})q_{U_{3}}^{n}(u_{3}^{n})p_{V_{1}}^{n}(v_{1}^{n})p_{V_{2}}^{n}(v_{2}^{n})p_{V_{3}}^{n}(v_{3}^{n})

\displaystyle r_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n},\underline{v}^{n}\right)\tr\left\{\left(I-\Gamma^{Y_{2}}_{m_{2}}\right)\rho_{f^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n},\underline{v}^{n}\right)}^{Y_{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\frac{1}{2^{n(S_{2}+S_{3}+K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3})}}\sum_{\underline{m}}\sum_{a_{2},a_{3}}\sum_{b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}}\sum_{u_{2}^{n}}\sum_{u_{3}^{n}}\sum_{\underline{v}^{n}}p_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}(u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n},\underline{v}^{n})\tr\left\{\left(I-\Gamma^{Y_{2}}_{m_{2}}\right)\rho_{f^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n},\underline{v}^{n}\right)}^{Y_{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{2}+S_{2}+K_{2})}}\sum_{m_{2}}\sum_{a_{2}}\sum_{b_{2}}\sum_{u_{2}^{n}}\sum_{v_{2}^{n}}p_{U_{2}V_{2}}^{n}(u_{2}^{n},v_{2}^{n})\tr\left\{\left(I-\Gamma^{Y_{2}}_{m_{2}}\right)\rho_{u_{2}^{n},v_{2}^{n}}^{Y_{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{2}+S_{2}+K_{2})}}\sum_{m_{2}}\sum_{a_{2}}\sum_{b_{2}}\sum_{u_{2}^{n}}\sum_{v_{2}^{n}}p_{U_{2}V_{2}}^{n}(u_{2}^{n},v_{2}^{n})

\displaystyle\left[\tr\left\{\left(I-\Gamma^{Y_{2}}_{m_{2}}\right)\>\pi^{Y_{2}}_{v_{2}^{n}}\>\rho_{u_{2}^{n},v_{2}^{n}}^{Y_{2}}\>\pi^{Y_{2}}_{v_{2}^{n}}\right\}+\norm{ \: \pi^{Y_2}_{v_2^n} \: \rho_{u_2^n, v_2^n}^{Y_2} \: \pi^{Y_2}_{v_2^n} - \rho_{u_2^n, v_2^n}^{Y_2} }_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{2}+S_{2}+K_{2})}}\sum_{m_{2}}\sum_{a_{2}}\sum_{b_{2}}\sum_{u_{2}^{n}}\sum_{v_{2}^{n}}p_{U_{2}V_{2}}^{n}(u_{2}^{n},v_{2}^{n})\tr\left\{\left(I-\Gamma^{Y_{2}}_{m_{2}}\right)\>\pi^{Y_{2}}_{v_{2}^{n}}\>\rho_{u_{2}^{n},v_{2}^{n}}^{Y_{2}}\>\pi^{Y_{2}}_{v_{2}^{n}}\right\}+2\sqrt{\epsilon},

where (a) follows from
q_{U_{2}}^{n}(u_{2}^{n})q_{U_{3}}^{n}(u_{3}^{n})p_{V_{1}}^{n}(v_{1}^{n})p_{V_{2}}^{n}(v_{2}^{n})p_{V_{3}}^{n}(v_{3}^{n})r_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n},\underline{v}^{n}\right)=p_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}(u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n},\underline{v}^{n})
, (b) follows from

\rho^{Y_{2}}_{u_{2}^{n},v_{2}^{n}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\sum_{v_{1}^{n},u_{3}^{n},v_{3}^{n}}p_{V_{1}U_{3}V_{3}|U_{2}V_{2}}^{n}(v_{1}^{n},u_{3}^{n},v_{3}^{n}|u_{2}^{n},v_{2}^{n})\rho^{Y_{2}}_{f^{n}(u_{2}^{n},u_{3}^{n},\underline{v}^{n})}
, (c) follows from the inequality
\tr(\Delta\rho)\leq\tr(\Delta\sigma)+\frac{1}{2}\norm{\rho- \sigma}_{1}
, for
0\leq\Delta,\rho,\sigma\leq I
, and (d) follows from
\tr(\pi^{Y_2}_{v_2^n} \rho_{u_2^n,v_2^n}^{Y_2})\geq 1-\epsilon
,
and from the Gentle Operator Lemma
[
25
]
, for sufficiently large
n
.
Applying Hayashi-Nagaoka inequality
[
34
]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[T_{2.2}]\leq 2\>T_{2.2.1}+4\left(\sum_{i=2}^{9}T_{2.2.i}\right)+2\sqrt{\epsilon},\mbox{ where}

\displaystyle T_{2.2.1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{2}+S_{2}+K_{2})}}\sum_{m_{2}}\sum_{a_{2}}\sum_{b_{2}}\sum_{u_{2}^{n}}\sum_{v_{2}^{n}}p_{U_{2}V_{2}}^{n}(u_{2}^{n},v_{2}^{n})\tr\left\{\left(I-\Upsilon_{u_{2}^{n},v_{2}^{n}}\right)\>\pi^{Y_{2}}_{v_{2}^{n}}\>\rho_{u_{2}^{n},v_{2}^{n}}^{Y_{2}}\>\pi^{Y_{2}}_{v_{2}^{n}}\right\},

\displaystyle T_{2.2.2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{2}+S_{2}+K_{2})}}\sum_{m_{2}}\sum_{a_{2}}\sum_{b_{2}}\sum_{\tilde{m}_{22}\neq m_{22}}\sum_{\tilde{b}_{2}}\sum_{u_{2}^{n}}\sum_{v_{2}^{n}}p_{U_{2}V_{2}}^{n}(u_{2}^{n},v_{2}^{n})\tr\left\{\Upsilon_{u_{2}^{n},V_{2}^{n}(\tilde{b}_{2},\tilde{m}_{22})}\>\pi^{Y_{2}}_{v_{2}^{n}}\>\rho_{u_{2}^{n},v_{2}^{n}}^{Y_{2}}\>\pi^{Y_{2}}_{v_{2}^{n}}\right\},

V
New Inner Bound to the Capacity Region of the
3-
CQBC using Coset Codes

We begin by stating some common notation that we employ throughout this section, followed by a discussion of our goals.

Notation 2
.

In any context, when used in conjunction,
i,j,k
will denote distinct indices in
[3]
, hence
\{i,j,k\}=[3]
and we let
\llbracket 3\rrbracket\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\{(1,2),(1,3),(2,1),(2,3),(3,1),(3,2)\}
.

Our goal in this section is to design and analyze a general coding strategy that enables
each
Rx decode
efficiently

both
bivariate and univariate interference components that affects its reception. As we discussed in Sec.
III-B
, unstructured IID codes and jointly designed coset codes enable efficient decoding of univariate and bivariate interference components respectively. Analogous to how Marton
[
12
]
combined Cover’s superposition coding
[
21
]
with her precoding via binning
[
44
,
12
]
technique through a two layer code, our general coding strategy will also involve two layers - one built using jointly designed coset codes to enable efficient decoding of bivariate interference components and the second involving unstructured IID codes. Refer to Fig.
5
for a depiction of the random variables involved in our general coding strategy.
W,Q_{12},Q_{23},Q_{31},V_{1},V_{2},V_{3}
constitute the unstructured IID codes employed in the natural generalization of Marton’s coding strategy to the
3-
CQBC. See
[
27
, Thm. 2 in Sec. II.D]
. To this, we append the layer of coset codes represented via random variables
U_{ij}:(i,j)\in\llbracket 3\rrbracket
. As we will elaborate in Sec.
V-A
,
U_{ij}
and
U_{kj}
are supported on the same finite field
\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon_{j}}
permitting the decoding of
U_{ij}\oplus U_{kj}
. The codebooks decoded into by each Rx is indicated by the corresponding random variables depicted on the right.

We present this general coding strategy and the corresponding inner bound in two pedagogical steps. Step II, presented in Sec.
V-A
will present, design and analysis of only the layer involving coset codes. Viewed
through the lens of Fig.
5
, the Step II coding strategy corresponds to random variables
W=Q_{12}=Q_{23}=Q_{31}=\phi
being trivial. In Step III, presented in Sec.
V-B
, we combine this Step II design with a base layer involving unstructured IID codes, as depicted via random variables
W,Q_{12},Q_{23},Q_{31}
in Fig.
5
. Lemma
1
proves that the inner bound characterized in Thm.
2
can be strictly larger for certain examples. Our analysis of the coding strategy presented in Step III yields an inner bound that, in addition to be strictly larger for certain examples, subsumes all previous known inner bounds. See Rem.
6
.

Depiction of all random variables in the full blown coding strategy, where,
U_{j*}=(U_{ji},U_{jk})
for
j\in[3]
, . In Sec.

(Step II) only random variables in the gray dashed box are non-trivial, with the rest trivial.

V-A

Step II : A Coset Code Strategy to manage Bivariate Interference at all Rxs over a
3-
CQBC

In this step, we design and analyze a coding strategy that enables
each

employed jointly designed coset code to assist Rx

Refer to Fig.
5
. In this second step, we activate only the ‘bivariate’
\underline{U}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(U_{1*},U_{2*},U_{3*})
and private parts
\underline{V}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(V_{1},V_{2},V_{3})
choosing them to be non-trivial, with the rest
W=Q_{12}=Q_{23}=Q_{31}=\phi
trivial. The coding strategy and its analysis presented in the proof of Thm.
3
involves simultaneous decoding by the Rxs of a combination of unstructured IID codes and jointly designed coset codes, including decoding of the field addition and hence addresses all the new elements.

Proof.

\mathcal{U}_{ji}=\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon_{i}}

ji\in\llbracket 3\rrbracket
, and
K_{j},L_{j}
, for
j\in[3]
such that
\mathbb{E}[\kappa(X)]\leq\tau
,
R_{j}=T_{ji}+T_{jk}+L_{j}
for
ji\in\llbracket 3\rrbracket
, and the bounds in (
24
)-(
32
) hold.
We divide the proof into four parts - code structure, encoding, decoding POVMs, and error analysis. Since the error analysis at each Rx is essentially the error analysis of an effective
4-
user classical-quantum multiple access channel (
4-
CQMAC), we present the error analysis only for this
4-
CQMAC.

Code structure:

For
j\in[3]
, Rx
j
’s message is split into three parts
m_{j}=(m_{ji},m_{jk},m_{jj})
. See Fig.
6
. The semi private parts
m_{ji}
and
m_{jk}
are communicated using coset codes built over
\mathcal{U}_{ji}
and
\mathcal{U}_{jk}
respectively. The private part
m_{jj}
is communicated using a conventional IID random code built over
\mathcal{V}_{j}
.
U_{j*}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(U_{ji},U_{jk})
represent information about the semi private parts
(m_{ji},m_{jk})
and
V_{j}
represents information about the private part
m_{jj}
. Note that
(U_{ji},U_{jk},V_{j})\in\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon_{i}}\times\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon_{k}}\times\mathcal{V}_{j}
.
Specifically, the message
m_{jj}
is communicated using (i)
c_{jj}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\{v_{j}^{n}(b_{j},m_{jj}):b_{j}\in[2^{nK_{j}}],m_{jj}\in[2^{nL_{j}}]\}
constructed over
\mathcal{V}_{j}
, and (ii) a binning map
b_{j}:[2^{nR_{1}}]\times[2^{nR_{2}}]\times[2^{nR_{3}}]\rightarrow[2^{nK_{j}}]
. Let
v^{n}_{j}(b_{j}(\underline{m}),m_{jj})
denote the chosen codeword from the bin
c_{jj}(m_{jj})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\{v_{j}^{n}(b_{j},m_{jj}):b_{j}\in[2^{nK_{j}}]\}
corresponding to message
m_{jj}
.
For
ji\in\llbracket 3\rrbracket
, the message
m_{ji}
is communicated using (i) a NCC over the finite field
\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon_{i}}
denoted by
(n,s_{ji},t_{ji},g^{ji}_{I},g^{ji}_{O/I},b_{ji}^{n})
where
g^{ji}_{I}\in\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon_{i}}^{s_{ji}\times n}
,
g^{ji}_{O/I}\in\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon_{i}}^{t_{ji}\times n}
,
b_{ji}^{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon_{i}}^{n}
,
s_{ji}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\left\lceil\frac{nS_{ji}}{\log(\Prime_i)}\right\rceil
and
t_{ji}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\left\lfloor\frac{nT_{ji}}{\log(\Prime_i)}\right\rfloor
, and (ii) a binning map
a_{ji}:[2^{nR_{1}}]\times[2^{nR_{2}}]\times[2^{nR_{3}}]\rightarrow[2^{nS_{ji}}]
. We emphasize that the coset codes built over
\mathcal{U}_{ij}
and
\mathcal{U}_{kj}
intersect. In other words, the smaller of these two codes is a sub-coset of the larger. This is accomplished by ensuring that the rows of the generator matrix of the larger code contain all the rows of the generator matrix of the smaller code i.e for
j\in[3]
the generator matrices
g_{ij}=\left[\!\!\begin{array}[]{c}g_{I}^{ij}\\
g_{O/I}^{ij}\end{array}\!\!\right]
satisfy
g_{\mathsf{b}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\left[\!\!\begin{array}[]{c}g_{\mathsf{s}}\\
g_{\Delta}\end{array}\!\!\right]
where
\mathsf{b},\mathsf{s}
are defined such that

\{\mathsf{b},\mathsf{s}\}=\{ij,kj\}\mbox{ and }s_{\mathsf{b}}+t_{\mathsf{b}}\geq s_{\mathsf{s}}+t_{\mathsf{s}}
.

codes employed in the coding strategy are depicted in the grey box above. The

are coset codes built over finite fields. Codes with the same color are built over the same finite field, and the smaller of the two is a sub-coset of the larger. The black codes are built over auxiliary finite sets
\mathcal{V}_{j}

U_{21}
and
U_{31}
codes.

For
ji\in\llbracket 3\rrbracket
, let
u_{ji}^{n}(a_{ji}(\underline{m}),m_{ji})
denote the chosen codeword from the coset
c_{ji}(m_{ji})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\{u_{ji}^{n}(a_{ji},m_{ji}):a_{ji}\in\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon_{i}}^{s_{ji}}\}
, where
u_{ji}^{n}(a_{ji},m_{ji})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}a_{ji}g_{I}^{ji}\oplus m_{ji}g_{O|I}^{ji}\oplus b_{ji}^{n}
. Finally, let
c\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\{x^{n}(\underline{m}):\underline{m}\in[2^{nR_{1}}]\times[2^{nR_{2}}]\times[2^{nR_{3}}]\}
be a codebook constructed over
\mathcal{X}
.

Encoding Rule:

To communicate the message triple
\underline{m}=(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})
, the encoder prepares the quantum state
\rho^{Y_{1}Y_{2}Y_{3}}_{x^{n}(\underline{m})}
.

Decoding POVM:
For
j\in[3],
Rx
j
decodes a bivariate component
u^{n}_{ij}(a_{ij}(\underline{m}),m_{ij})\oplus u^{n}_{kj}(a_{kj}(\underline{m}),m_{kj})
in addition to the codewords
u^{n}_{ji}(a_{ji}(\underline{m}),m_{ji}),u^{n}_{jk}(a_{jk}(\underline{m}),m_{jk}),v^{n}_{j}(b_{j}(\underline{m}),m_{jj})
corresponding to its own message. Specifically, Rx
j
attempt to decode the quartet

\displaystyle\left(u^{n}_{ji}(a_{ji}(\underline{m}),m_{ji}),u^{n}_{jk}(a_{jk}(\underline{m}),m_{jk}),v^{n}_{j}(b_{j}(\underline{m}),m_{jj}),u^{n}_{ij}(a_{ij}(\underline{m}),m_{ij})\oplus u^{n}_{kj}(a_{kj}(\underline{m}),m_{kj})\right)

using simultaneous decoding. In particular, the construction of our simultaneous decoding POVM adopts the
tilting, smoothing, and augmentation
technique of Sen
[
24
]
. A careful consideration of this setting reveals that if one considers this setting carefully from a receiver’s channel coding error event, it will be evident that communication is occurring over an induced
4-
CQMAC. We now proceed towards identifying this induced
4-
CQMAC.

\displaystyle\xi^{U_{j*}V_{j}U_{j}^{\oplus}Y_{j}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{u_{j*},v_{j},u_{j}^{\oplus}}p_{U_{j*}V_{j}U_{j}^{\oplus}}(u_{j*},v_{j},u_{j}^{\oplus})\outerproduct{u_{j*}~v_{j}~u_{j}^{\oplus}}{u_{j*}~v_{j}~u_{j}^{\oplus}}\otimes\xi_{u_{j*},v_{j},u_{j}^{\oplus}},\mbox{ where }

(33)

\displaystyle\xi_{u_{j*}v_{j}u_{j}^{\oplus}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{u_{i*}}\sum_{u_{k*}}\sum_{v_{i}}\sum_{v_{k}}p_{U_{i*}U_{k*}V_{i}V_{k}|U_{j*}V_{j}U_{j}^{\oplus}}(u_{i*},u_{k*},v_{i},v_{k}|u_{j*},v_{j},u_{j}^{\oplus})\rho^{Y_{j}}_{f(\underline{u},\underline{v})}.

Rx
j
’s error analysis is essentially the error analysis of the above MAC, where Rx

attempts to decode

, and

and

Fig. 8:
Mapping between the new notation and the original notation

To analyze the 4
-
CQMAC, we adopt Sen’s
[
24
]
technique of
tilting, smoothing, and augmentation
. In this setting, there are
15=2^{4}-1
possible error events, each corresponding to a different subspace, and these subspaces can overlap in a nontrivial way. Sen’s
[
24
]
idea is to enlarge the space so that we can tilt each error subspace in an orthogonal direction and increase their separation. We also augment the space to ensure the smoothness property of the states obtained by averaging.
Following this approach, we define
14=(2^{4}-1)-1
tilting maps, one for each error subspace except the last, where all four messages
\underline{m}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3},m_{4})
are wrong and remains untilted. We also define an additional tilting map for the quantum state itself, which tilts the state slightly in all directions. This tilting map should be chosen carefully so that the tilted state remains close to the original state in
\mathbb{L}_{1}
norm and satisfies the required smoothing properties, that is, the difference between the average of the original state and the average of the tilted state
4
4

4

When we say ‘average of
\cdots
’ we mean the expectation of the tilted state over the random codebook.
is small.
In what follows, we present this approach in detail. First, we define the new enlarged and augmented space. Next, we define the tilting maps. Then, we define the tilted state and state how close it remains to the original state. Finally, we describe the tilted subspace and construct the final POVM, which can simultaneously decode all messages.

.

S

(38)

Distribution of the random code:
As is standard in information theory, to derive an upper bound on the average error probability, we average over the ensemble of all codes.
The distribution of the random code is analogous to (
20
), that is employed in step I analysis involving
5
codes. This is naturally extended to the scenarios involving
9
codes, so in essence the unstructured codebooks are picked independently, and the coset codes are picked such that the larger of the codes in the same field contains the smaller one. For more elaboration, please look at the distribution (
20
) in step I.

Error Analysis:

Based on the exposition provided in Remark
2
,we recognize that our approach enables us to separate the binning analysis and the channel coding analysis, thereby breaking the independence. We therefore assume no binning in the following analysis.
As is standard in information theory, we derive an upper-bound on the probability of error of a good code by averaging the error probability over an ensemble of codes. Towards that end , we first introduce an alternate ‘proxy’ state. Specifically, we substitute the state
\xi_{\underline{z}^{n}}^{\otimes n}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}
by a specific ‘tilted state’ defined as

\displaystyle\theta_{\underline{z}^{n},\underline{d}^{n}}^{\otimes n}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathcal{T}_{\underline{d}^{n},\eta}\{\xi_{\underline{z}^{n}}^{\otimes n}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}\},

where the tilting map
\mathcal{T}_{\underline{d}^{n},\eta}
acts on each pure state in the mixture individually and is defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\underline{d}^{n},\eta}(\ket{h})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Omega(\eta)}}\left(\ket{h}+\sum_{S\subsetneq[4]}\eta^{|S|}\ket{h}\otimes\ket{d_{S}^{n}}\right)

with
\Omega(\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}1+16\eta^{2}+36\eta^{4}+16\eta^{6}
.
The effect of this substitution on the error probability can be suppressed by ensuring that the tilted state
\theta_{\underline{z}^{n},\underline{d}^{n}}^{\otimes n}
is close to the state
\xi_{\underline{z}^{n}}^{\otimes n}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}
in the
\mathbb{L}_{1}-
norm, as established in the following Proposition.

Proposition 11
.

For
n
sufficiently large, we have

\displaystyle\norm{\theta_{\ulinez^{n}, \ulined^{n}}^{\otimes n} - \left(\xi_{\ulinez^{n}}^{\otimes n} \otimes\ket{0}\bra{0}\right) }_{1}\leq 12\eta.

(39)

Proof.

The proof is analogous to the proof in Appendix
C
.
∎

We decompose the average probability of error of the 4-user CQMAC (Fig.
8
) as

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(\underline{e},\mu)=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\sum_{\underline{m}}\tr\left\{\left(I-\mu_{\underline{m}}\right)\left(\xi_{\underline{z}}^{n}(\underline{m})\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}\right)\right)\leq T_{1}+T_{2},

where the inequality follows from using the inequality
\tr\left(\Delta\rho\right)\leq\tr\left(\Delta\sigma\right)+\frac{1}{2}\norm{\rho-\sigma}_{1}
, with
0\leq\Lambda,\rho,\sigma\leq I
, which gives us

\displaystyle T_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\sum_{\underline{m}}\tr\left\{\left(I-\mu_{\underline{m}}\right)\theta_{\underline{z}^{n}(\underline{m}),\underline{d}^{n}(\underline{m})}\right),\mbox{ and }T_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\sum_{\underline{m}}\norm{\theta_{\ulinez^n(\ulinem), \ulined^n(\ulinem)}-\left(\xi_{\ulinez^n(\ulinem)} \otimes\ketbra{0}\right)}_{1}.

We analyze the first term
T_{1}
, using the Hayashi–Nagaoka inequality
[
34
]
. This allows us to decompose
T_{1}
as

\displaystyle T_{1}=2\>T_{1.1}+4\sum_{S\subseteq[4]}T_{1.S},\mbox{ where}

\displaystyle T_{1.1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\sum_{\underline{m}}\tr\left\{(I-\gamma^{*}_{\underline{z}^{n}(\underline{m}),\underline{d}^{n}(\underline{m})})\theta_{\underline{z}^{n}(\underline{m}),\underline{d}^{n}(\underline{m})}\right),\mbox{ and}

\displaystyle T_{1.S}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\sum_{\underline{m}}\sum_{\tilde{m}_{S}\neq m_{S}}\tr\left\{\gamma^{*}_{z_{S}^{n}(\tilde{m}_{S}),d^{n}_{S}(\tilde{m}_{S}),z^{n}_{S^{c}}(m_{S^{c}}),d_{S^{c}}^{n}(m_{S^{c}})}\theta_{\underline{z}^{n}(\underline{m}),\underline{d}^{n}(\underline{m})}\right).

The following propositions summarize all the rate constraints that result from bounding these error terms.

Proposition 12
.

For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, and for all sufficiently small
\delta,\eta>0
, and sufficiently large
n
, we have
\mathbb{E}[T_{1.1}]\leq\epsilon
.

Proof.

The proof is provided in Appendix
H
.
∎

Proposition 13
.

For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, and for all sufficiently small
\delta,\eta>0
, and sufficiently large
n
, we have
\mathbb{E}[T_{1.S}]\leq\epsilon,
if

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in S}\tilde{R}_{s}<I(Y;Z_{S}|Z_{S^{c}}),\mbox{ for }S\subseteq[4].

Proof.

The proof is provided in Appendix
I
.
∎

Next, from Proposition
11
, we have for all
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and for
\eta=\epsilon^{\frac{1}{5}}
,
\mathbb{E}[T_{2}]\leq 12\>\epsilon^{\frac{1}{5}}
. The choice of
\eta
ensures that
\mathbb{E}[T_{1.1}]
can be made arbitrarily small, as shown in the proof of Proposition
12
.
Based on Remark
2
and our exposition in the proof in Sec.
IV
, specifically the separation of binning and channel coding, this proof is complete.
∎

V-B

Step III: Enlarging
\alpha_{S}
via Unstructured IID codes to
\alpha_{US}

The inner bound proven in Thm.
3
only enables Rxs to decode a bivariate component of the interference. As Marton’s common codebook
[
12
]
strategy proves, there is import to decoding a univariate part of the other user’s codewords. In other words, by combining both unstructured codebook based strategy and coset code strategy, we can derive an inner bound that subsumes all current known inner bounds for the
3-
CQBC. The following inner bound is obtained by combining the conventional unstructured IID code based strategy due to Marton
[
12
]
and the above coset code based strategy (Thm.
3
).
A proof of this inner bound is involved, but essentially is a generalization of the proof provided for Thm.
3
. In regards to the code structure, refer to Fig.
9
.

Throughout the statement of Thm.
4
, we adopt the following notation.

Fig. 9:
13
codes employed in the proof of Thm.
4
. The
U_{ji}:ji\in\llbracket 3\rrbracket
are coset codes built over finite fields. Codes with the same color are built over the same finite field and the smaller of the two is a sub-coset of the larger. The black, yellow and the orange codes are built over auxiliary finite sets
\mathcal{W}:ji\in\llbracket 3\rrbracket,\mathcal{Q}_{ji}:ji\in\{12,23,31\},
and
\mathcal{V}_{j}:j\in[3]
respectively using the conventional IID random code structure.

Notation 4
.

As stated earlier,
i,j,k
will denote distinct indices in
[3]
, hence
\{i,j,k\}=[3]
and we let
\llbracket 3\rrbracket\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\{(1,2),(1,3),(2,1),(2,3),(3,1),(3,2)\}
. In addition,
\alpha,\beta,\iota
will denote distinct indices in
\{1,2,3\}
satisfying
(\alpha,\beta,\iota)\in\{(1,2,3),(2,3,1),(3,1,2)\}
.
\underline{Q}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(Q_{12},Q_{23},Q_{31})
and similarly
\underline{\mathcal{Q}}=\mathcal{Q}_{12}\times\mathcal{Q}_{23}\times\mathcal{Q}_{31}
,
\underline{q}=(q_{12},q_{23},q_{31})
, and analogously the rest of the objects.

Theorem 4
.

Refer to the above notation. Let
\hat{\alpha}_{US}\in[0,\infty)^{4}
be the set of all rate-cost quadruples
(R_{1},R_{2},R_{3},\tau)\in[0,\infty)^{4}
for which there exists (i) finite sets
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{Q}_{12},\mathcal{Q}_{23},\mathcal{Q}_{31},\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{2},\mathcal{V}_{3}
, (ii) finite fields
\mathcal{U}_{ij}=\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon_{j}}
for each
ij\in\llbracket 3\rrbracket
, (ii) a PMF
p_{W\underline{Q}\underline{U}~\!\!\underline{V}X}=p_{WQ_{12}Q_{23}Q_{31}U_{1*}U_{2*}U_{3*}V_{1}V_{2}V_{3}X}
on
\underline{\mathcal{U}}\times\underline{Q}\times\underline{\mathcal{V}}\times\mathcal{X}
, (iii) nonnegative numbers
\zeta_{j},K_{j},L_{j}:j\in[3]

\eta_{\alpha\beta},\nu_{\alpha\beta},\psi_{\alpha\beta}
for
(\alpha,\beta,\iota)\in\{(1,2,3),(2,3,1),(3,1,2)\},S_{ij},T_{ij}:ij\in\llbracket 3\rrbracket
, such that
R_{j}=\zeta_{j}+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{3}\left(\nu_{\alpha\beta}+\eta_{\iota\alpha}\right)\mathds{1}_{\{\alpha=j\}}+T_{ji}+T_{jk}+L_{j}
for
j\in[3]
,
\mathbb{E}[\kappa(X)]\leq\tau
,

\displaystyle S_{A}+M_{B}+K_{C}+\psi_{D}>\Theta(A,B,C,D),\mbox{ where}

\displaystyle\Theta(A,B,C,D)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\theta_{j}:j\in B)\in\underset{j\in B}{\prod}\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon_{j}}\end{subarray}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\sum_{a\in A}\log|\mathcal{U}_{a}|+\sum_{j\in B}\log\upsilon_{j}+\sum_{c\in C}H(V_{c}|W)+\sum_{d\in D}H(Q_{D}|W)-\\
H(U_{A},U_{ij}\oplus\theta_{j}U_{kj}\!:j\in B,V_{C}|W)\end{array}\right\}

for all

, that satisfy
A\cap A(B)=\phi
, where

,

,

,

,

,
\psi_{D}=\sum_{d\in D}\psi_{d}
and

\displaystyle S_{\mathcal{A}_{j}}+T_{\mathcal{A}_{j}}+\psi_{\mathcal{C}_{j}}+\nu_{\mathcal{C}_{j}}+\eta_{\mathcal{C}_{j}}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{j}}\log|\mathcal{U}_{a}|+H(Q_{\mathcal{C}_{j}}|Q_{\mathcal{C}_{j}^{C}},W)

(42)

\displaystyle-H(U_{\mathcal{A}_{j}},Q_{\mathcal{C}_{j}}|U_{\mathcal{A}_{j}^{c}},Q_{\mathcal{C}_{j}^{C}},U_{ij}\oplus U_{kj},V_{j},Y_{j},W),

u_{1}^{\oplus},u_{2}^{\oplus},u_{3}^{\oplus}\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!p_{W\underline{Q}~\!\!\underline{U}~\!\!\underline{V}X}(w,\underline{q},\underline{u},\underline{v},x)\mathds{1}{\left\{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{ij}\oplus u_{kj}=u_{j}^{\oplus}:j\in[3]\end{subarray}\right\}}\!\outerproduct{w~\ulineq~\ulineu~ u_{1}^{\oplus}~ u_{2}^{\oplus}~ u_{3}^{\oplus}~ \ulinev~ x}{w~\ulineq~\ulineu~ u_{1}^{\oplus}~ u_{2}^{\oplus}~ u_{3}^{\oplus}~ \ulinev~ x}\!\otimes\!\rho_{x}.

Let
\alpha_{US}
denote the convex closure of
\hat{\alpha}_{US}
. Then
\alpha_{US}\subseteq\mathscr{C}(\tau)
is an achievable rate region.

Remark 6
.

By choosing
U_{ji}=\phi,
for
(i,j)\in\llbracket 3\rrbracket,
we recover the best known inner bound achievable using unstructured IID codes.

Remark 7
.

The above inner bound is characterized for a
3-
CQBC. We can extend this via the standard technique of regularization
[
25
, Sec. 20.1, Eq. 20.7]
to characterize an inner bound to the bit communicating capacity region of a general
3-
user quantum broadcast channel. We omit a mathematical characterization of this inner bound in the interest of brevity.

Appendix A
Proof of Proposition
1
and
2

A-A

Proof of Proposition
1

Analysis of
T_{j.2.1}
:
We evaluate the expectation of
T_{j.2.1}
over the codebook generation distribution. We obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[T_{j.2.1}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\frac{1}{2^{n(K_{1}+K_{2})}}\sum_{m_{1}}\sum_{m_{2}}\sum_{b_{1}}\sum_{b_{2}}\sum_{v_{1}^{n}}\sum_{v_{2}^{n}}q_{V_{1}}^{n}(v_{1}^{n})q_{V_{2}}^{n}(v_{2}^{n})r_{V_{1}V_{2}}^{n}(v_{1}^{n},v_{2}^{n})\tr\left\{\left(I^{Y_{j}}-\pi\>\pi_{v_{j}^{n}}\>\pi\right)\rho_{f^{n}(v_{1}^{n},v_{2}^{n})}^{Y_{j}}\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\frac{1}{2^{n(K_{1}+K_{2})}}\sum_{m_{1}}\sum_{m_{2}}\sum_{b_{1}}\sum_{b_{2}}\sum_{v_{1}^{n}}\sum_{v_{2}^{n}}p_{V_{1}V_{2}}^{n}(v_{1}^{n},v_{2}^{n})\tr\left\{\left(I^{Y_{j}}-\pi\>\pi_{v_{j}^{n}}\>\pi\right)\rho_{f^{n}(v_{1}^{n},v_{2}^{n})}^{Y_{j}}\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{j}+K_{j})}}\sum_{m_{j}}\sum_{b_{j}}\sum_{v_{j}^{n}}p_{V_{j}}^{n}(v_{j}^{n})\tr\left\{\left(I^{Y_{j}}-\pi\>\pi_{v_{j}^{n}}\>\pi\right)\rho_{v_{j}^{n}}^{Y_{j}}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{j}+K_{j})}}\sum_{m_{j}}\sum_{b_{j}}\sum_{v_{j}^{n}\notin T_{\delta}^{n}(p_{V_{j}})}p_{V_{j}}^{n}(v_{j}^{n})+\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{j}+K_{j})}}\sum_{m_{j}}\sum_{b_{j}}\sum_{v_{j}^{n}\in T_{\delta}^{n}(p_{V_{j}})}p_{V_{j}}^{n}(v_{j}^{n})\tr\left\{\left(I^{Y_{j}}-\pi\>\pi_{v_{j}^{n}}\>\pi\right)\rho_{v_{j}^{n}}^{Y_{j}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon+\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{j}+K_{j})}}\sum_{m_{j}}\sum_{b_{j}}\sum_{v_{j}^{n}\in T_{\delta}^{n}(p_{V_{j}})}p_{V_{j}}^{n}(v_{j}^{n})\tr\left\{\left(I^{Y_{j}}-\pi\>\pi_{v_{j}^{n}}\>\pi\right)\rho_{v_{j}^{n}}^{Y_{j}}\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}

\displaystyle 2\epsilon+2\sqrt{\epsilon},

where (a) follows from (
2
), (b) follows from the definition of
\rho_{v_{j}^{n}}^{Y_{j}}
, and (c) follows from

\displaystyle\tr\left\{\pi\pi_{v_{j}^{n}}\pi\rho_{v_{j}^{n}}^{Y_{j}}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tr\left\{\pi_{v_{j}^{n}}\pi\rho_{v_{j}^{n}}^{Y_{j}}\pi\right\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\tr\left\{\pi_{v_{j}^{n}}\rho_{v_{j}^{n}}^{Y_{j}}\right\}-\norm{\pi\rho_{v_j^n}^{Y_j} \pi- \rho_{v_j^n}^{Y_j}}_{1}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1-\epsilon-2\sqrt{\epsilon},

where the first equality follows from the cyclicity of the trace, first inequality follows from using
\tr(\Delta\rho)\geq\tr(\lambda\sigma)-\norm{\rho- \sigma}_{1}
for
0\leq\Delta,\rho,\sigma\leq I
and second inequality follows from (i) the typical property
\tr\left\{\pi_{v_{j}^{n}}\rho_{v_{j}^{n}}^{Y_{j}}\right\}\geq 1-\epsilon
, for
v_{j}^{n}\in T_{\delta}^{n}(p_{V_{j}})
and from (ii) the gentle measurement operator
[
25
]
.

A-B

Proof of Proposition
2

Analysis of
T_{j.2.2}
:
We evaluate the expectation of
T_{j.2.2}
over the codebook generation distribution. We obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[T_{j.2.2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\frac{1}{2^{n(K_{1}+K_{2})}}\sum_{m_{1}}\sum_{m_{2}}\sum_{b_{1}}\sum_{b_{2}}\sum_{\tilde{m}_{j}\neq m_{j}}\sum_{\tilde{b}_{j}}\sum_{\tilde{v}_{j}^{n}}\sum_{v_{1}^{n}}\sum_{v_{2}^{n}}q_{V_{j}}^{n}(\tilde{v}_{j}^{n})\>q_{V_{1}}^{n}(v_{1}^{n})\>q_{V_{2}}^{n}(v_{2}^{n})\>r_{V_{1}V_{2}}^{n}(v_{1}^{n},v_{2}^{n})

(51)

\displaystyle\tr\left\{\pi\pi_{\tilde{v}_{j}^{n}}\pi\rho_{f^{n}(v_{1}^{n},v_{2}^{n})}^{Y_{j}}\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\frac{1}{2^{n(K_{1}+K_{2})}}\sum_{m_{1}}\sum_{m_{2}}\sum_{b_{1}}\sum_{b_{2}}\sum_{\tilde{m}_{j}\neq m_{j}}\sum_{\tilde{b}_{j}}\sum_{\tilde{v}_{j}^{n}}\sum_{v_{1}^{n}}\sum_{v_{2}^{n}}q_{V_{j}}^{n}(\tilde{v}_{j}^{n})\>p_{V_{1}V_{2}}^{n}(v_{1}^{n},v_{2}^{n})\>\tr\left\{\pi\pi_{\tilde{v}_{j}^{n}}\pi\rho_{f^{n}(v_{1}^{n},v_{2}^{n})}^{Y_{j}}\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}

\displaystyle\sum_{\tilde{m}_{j}\neq m_{j}}\sum_{\tilde{b}_{j}}\sum_{\tilde{v}_{j}^{n}\in T_{\delta}^{n}(p_{V_{j}})}q_{V_{j}}^{n}(\tilde{v}_{j}^{n})\>\tr\left\{\pi\pi_{\tilde{v}_{j}^{n}}\pi\left(\rho^{Y_{j}}\right)^{\otimes n}\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\sum_{\tilde{m}_{j}}\sum_{\tilde{b}_{j}}\sum_{\tilde{v}_{j}^{n}\in T_{\delta}^{n}(p_{V_{j}})}2^{-n(D(p_{V_{j}}||q_{V_{j}})-\delta_{j})}\>p_{V_{j}}^{n}(\tilde{v}_{j}^{n})\tr\left\{\pi_{\tilde{v}_{j}^{n}}\>\pi\left(\rho^{Y_{j}}\right)^{\otimes n}\pi\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\leq}

\displaystyle 2^{-n\left(I(V_{j};Y_{j})+D(p_{V_{j}}||q_{V_{j}})-2\delta-\delta_{j}-\left(R_{j}+K_{j}\right)\right)},

where (a) follows from (
2
), (b) follows from
\left(\rho^{Y_{j}}\right)^{\otimes n}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\otimes_{t=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{v_{1_{t}},v_{2_{t}}}p_{V_{1}V_{2}}(v_{1_{t}},v_{2_{t}})\rho_{f(v_{1_{t}},v_{2_{t}})}^{Y_{j}}\right)
and from
\pi_{\tilde{v}_{j}^{n}}=0,
for
\tilde{v}_{j}^{n}\notin T_{\delta}^{n}(p_{V_{j}})
,
(c) follows from (i) the cyclicity of the trace and from (ii)

\displaystyle q_{V_{j}}^{n}(\tilde{v}_{j}^{n})\leq 2^{-n\left(D(p_{V_{j}}||q_{V_{j}})-\delta_{j}\right)}\>p_{V_{j}}^{n}(\tilde{v}_{j}^{n}),\mbox{ for }\tilde{v}_{j}^{n}\in\mathcal{T}_{\delta}^{n}(p_{V_{j}})

(d) follows from

\displaystyle\tr\left\{\pi_{\tilde{v}_{j}^{n}}\pi\left(\rho^{Y_{j}}\right)^{\otimes n}\pi\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{-n(H(Y_{j})-\delta)}\tr\left\{\pi_{\tilde{v}_{j}^{n}}\pi\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{-n(H(Y_{j})-\delta)}\tr\left\{\pi_{\tilde{v}_{j}^{n}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{-n(H(Y_{j})-\delta)}2^{n(H(Y_{j}|V_{j})-\delta)},\mbox{ for }\tilde{v}_{j}^{n}\in T_{\delta}^{n}(p_{V_{j}}).

Hence,
T_{j.2.2}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, if
R_{j}+K_{j}<I(V_{j};Y_{j})+D(p_{V_{j}}||q_{V_{j}})
.

Analysis of
T_{j.2.3}
:
Following the identical steps as used in bounding
\mathbb{E}[T_{j.2.2}]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[T_{j.2.3}]\leq 2^{-n\left(I(V_{j};Y_{j})+D(p_{V_{j}}||q_{V_{j}})-2\delta-\delta_{j}-K_{j}\right)}.

Hence,
T_{j.2.3}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, if
K_{j}<I(V_{j};Y_{j})+D(p_{V_{j}}||q_{V_{j}})
.

and

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\begin{aligned} &\mathds{1}\left\{A_{2}(m_{21})=a_{2},A_{3}(m_{31})=a_{3},B_{1}(m_{1})=b_{1},B_{2}(m_{22})=b_{2},B_{3}(m_{33})=b_{3}\right\}-\\

&\frac{r_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}\left(U_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21}),U_{3}^{n}(a_{3},m_{31}),V_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),V_{2}^{n}(b_{2},m_{22}),V_{3}^{n}(b_{3},m_{33})\right)}{2^{n(S_{2}+S_{3}+K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3})}}\end{aligned}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{\underline{M}}|}\sum_{\underline{m}}\sum_{a_{2},a_{3}}\sum_{b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}}\sum_{c_{1}(m_{1})}\sum_{c_{2}(m_{21})}\sum_{c_{3}(m_{31})}\sum_{c_{2}(m_{22})}\sum_{c_{3}(m_{33})}

\displaystyle p\left(\begin{aligned} &c_{1}(m_{1})=\{v_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}):b_{1}\in[2^{nK_{1}}]\},c_{2}(m_{21})=\{u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21}):a_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon}^{s_{2}}\},\\
&c_{3}(m_{31})=\{u_{3}^{n}(a_{3},m_{31}):a_{3}\in\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon}^{s_{3}}\},\\

\displaystyle\left[\begin{aligned} &\frac{r_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21}),u_{3}^{n}(a_{3},m_{31}),v_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),v_{2}^{n}(b_{2},m_{22}),v_{3}^{n}(b_{3},m_{33})\right)}{\sum_{\tilde{a}_{2},\tilde{a}_{3}}\sum_{\tilde{b}_{1},\tilde{b}_{2},\tilde{b}_{3}}r_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n}(\tilde{a}_{2},m_{21}),u_{3}^{n}(\tilde{a}_{3},m_{31}),v_{1}^{n}(\tilde{b}_{1},m_{1}),v_{2}^{n}(\tilde{b}_{2},m_{22}),v_{3}^{n}(\tilde{b}_{3},m_{33})\right)}\\
&-\frac{r_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21}),u_{3}^{n}(a_{3},m_{31}),v_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),v_{2}^{n}(b_{2},m_{22}),v_{3}^{n}(b_{3},m_{33})\right)}{2^{n(S_{2}+S_{3}+K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3})}}\end{aligned}\right]

\displaystyle p\left(\begin{aligned} &c_{1}(m_{1})=\{v_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}):b_{1}\in[2^{nK_{1}}]\},c_{2}(m_{21})=\{u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21}):a_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon}^{s_{2}}\},\\
&c_{3}(m_{31})=\{u_{3}^{n}(a_{3},m_{31}):a_{3}\in\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon}^{s_{3}}\},c_{2}(m_{22})=\{v_{2}^{n}(b_{2},m_{22}):b_{2}\in[2^{nK_{2}}]\},\\
&c_{3}(m_{33})=\{v_{3}^{n}(b_{3},m_{33}):b_{3}\in[2^{nK_{3}}]\}\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\frac{r_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21}),u_{3}^{n}(a_{3},m_{31}),v_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}),v_{2}^{n}(b_{2},m_{22}),v_{3}^{n}(b_{3},m_{33})\right)}{\sum_{\tilde{a}_{2},\tilde{a}_{3}}\sum_{\tilde{b}_{1},\tilde{b}_{2},\tilde{b}_{3}}r_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n}(\tilde{a}_{2},m_{21}),u_{3}^{n}(\tilde{a}_{3},m_{31}),v_{1}^{n}(\tilde{b}_{1},m_{1}),v_{2}^{n}(\tilde{b}_{2},m_{22}),v_{3}^{n}(\tilde{b}_{3},m_{33})\right)}

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{\tilde{a}_{2},\tilde{a}_{3}}\sum_{\tilde{b}_{1},\tilde{b}_{2},\tilde{b}_{3}}\frac{r_{U_{2}U_{3}\underline{V}}^{n}\left(u_{2}^{n}(\tilde{a}_{2},m_{21}),u_{3}^{n}(\tilde{a}_{3},m_{31}),v_{1}^{n}(\tilde{b}_{1},m_{1}),v_{2}^{n}(\tilde{b}_{2},m_{22}),v_{3}^{n}(\tilde{b}_{3},m_{33})\right)}{2^{n(S_{2}+S_{3}+K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3})}}-1\right|

\displaystyle p\left(\begin{aligned} &c_{1}(m_{1})=\{v_{1}^{n}(b_{1},m_{1}):b_{1}\in[2^{nK_{1}}]\},c_{2}(m_{21})=\{u_{2}^{n}(a_{2},m_{21}):a_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{\upsilon}^{s_{2}}\},\\

&c_{2}(m_{22})=\{v_{2}^{n}(b_{2},m_{22}):b_{2}\in[2^{nK_{2}}]\},c_{3}(m_{33})=\{v_{3}^{n}(b_{3},m_{33}):b_{3}\in[2^{nK_{3}}]\}\end{aligned}\right)

]
, we have

if

\displaystyle>

for

,

,

,

,
